## Education

See also: [New Jersey Department of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Department_of_Education), [Post-secondary education in New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-secondary_education_in_New_Jersey), [List of high schools in New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_high_schools_in_New_Jersey), and [List of school districts in New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_school_districts_in_New_Jersey)



[Old Queens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Queens) at [Rutgers University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutgers_University), the largest state university system in New Jersey



[Cleveland Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleveland_Tower) at [Princeton University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princeton_University), an [Ivy League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivy_League) university and one of the world's most prominent research institutions, served briefly as the U.S. Capitol in the 18th century.

As of the 2020–2021 school year, there were 686 operating [districts in the state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_school_districts_in_New_Jersey). Of these, 599 were [traditional public school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_the_United_States) districts and 87 were [charter school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_schools_in_the_United_States) districts.[^cite_note-184][^cite_note-185] The [NJDOE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Department_of_Education) reported a total district enrollment of 1,362,400 students, the lowest total enrollment since the early 2000s, though these figures do not consider [homeschooled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeschooling) students or those attending [out-of-state schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boarding_school).[^cite_note-186] New Jersey public schools emphasize [STEM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/STEM) subjects, and New Jersey is home to more scientists and engineers per square mile than anywhere else in the world.[^cite_note-Nsci-87][^cite_note-Neng-187]

### Educational standards

New Jersey is known for the high quality of its education. In 2024, New Jersey spent the second-most per public school student among all states, behind [New York](8210131.md), amounting to \$26,600 spent per pupil.[^cite_note-PerPupil-188] In 2018, over 50% of the expenditure was allocated to student instruction.[^cite_note-189]

New Jersey is home to several prestigious private universities, including [Princeton University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princeton_University), one of the world's most prominent [research universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_university), which was ranked first for the tenth consecutive year among all national universities by *[U.S. News & World Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._News_&_World_Report)* in 2025 and has ranked first in comparable lists published by *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* and *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)*,[^cite_note-PNews-190][^cite_note-For1-191][^cite_note-WSJP-192] and public universities such as [Rutgers University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutgers_University), headquartered in [New Brunswick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Brunswick,_New_Jersey), [Middlesex County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middlesex_County,_New_Jersey), the state's flagship institution of higher education.[^cite_note-RutgersRef-193]

In 2019, 2020, and 2021, *Education Week* also ranked New Jersey public schools the best of all U.S. states.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-herb-195]

In 2016, nine New Jersey-based [high schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_education_in_the_United_States) were ranked among the top 25 in the nation, more than any other state, in "America's Top High Schools 2016", published by *[Newsweek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newsweek)*.[^cite_note-196]

In November 2023, New Jersey Governor [Phil Murphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Murphy) signed into law legislation eliminating testing for prospective teachers in reading, writing, and math, replacing it with an alternative certification process.[^cite_note-197]

## Economy

See also: [New Jersey locations by per capita income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_locations_by_per_capita_income)

According to the [U.S. Bureau of Economic Analysis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Bureau_of_Economic_Analysis), in 2025, New Jersey's [gross state product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_state_product) was \$887.1 billion and the state's per capita personal income was \$88,223.[^cite_note-198]

### Affluence



A heat map showing median income distribution in New Jersey by county

New Jersey's [per capita gross state product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_GDP_per_capita_\(nominal\)) routinely ranks among the highest in the nation. In 2020, New Jersey had more [millionaires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millionaire) both per capita and per square mile, representing roughly 9.76% of all New Jersey household, than any state in the nation.[^cite_note-Millionaires2-22]

The state is ranked second in the nation by the number of places with per capita incomes exceeding the national average with 76.4%. Three of New Jersey's counties are among the 20 highest income U.S. counties in the nation as of 2023.[^cite_note-199]

As of May 2025, the state's [unemployment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment) rate was 4.8%.[^cite_note-200]

### Fiscal policy

New Jersey has seven [tax brackets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_bracket) that determine [state income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_income_tax) rates, which range from 1.4% (for income below \$20,000) to 10.75% (for income above \$1,000,000).[^cite_note-201]

The standard [sales tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sales_tax) rate as of January 1, 2018, is 6.625%, applicable to all retail sales unless specifically exempt by law. This rate, which is comparably lower than that of New York City, often attracts numerous shoppers from New York City, often to suburban [Paramus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramus), New Jersey, which has five malls, one of which (the [Garden State Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garden_State_Plaza)) has over 2 million square feet (200,000 m<sup>2</sup>) of retail space. [Tax exemptions](960403.md) include most food items for at-home preparation, medications, most clothing, footwear and disposable paper products for use in the home.[^cite_note-202] There are 27 [Urban Enterprise Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_Enterprise_Zone) statewide, including sections of [Paterson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paterson,_New_Jersey), [Elizabeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth,_New_Jersey), and [Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City,_New_Jersey). In addition to other benefits to encourage employment within the zone, shoppers can take advantage of a reduced 3.3125% [sales tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sales_tax) rate (half the rate charged statewide) at eligible merchants.[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205]

New Jersey has the highest cumulative tax rate of all 50 states with residents paying a total of \$68 billion in state and local taxes annually with a per capita burden of \$7,816 at a rate of 12.9% of income.[^cite_note-206] All [real property](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_property) located in the state is subject to [property tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_tax) unless specifically exempted by statute. New Jersey does not assess an intangible personal property tax or an [estate tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estate_tax_in_the_United_States), but it does impose an [inheritance tax](5824627.md) (which is levied only on heirs who are not direct descendants).[^cite_note-207] In 2023, Governor [Phil Murphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Murphy) signed into law a new tax-relief program known as StayNJ that will provide for an annual property-tax cut of 50% for those aged 65 and over with incomes below \$500,000; the cut will go into effect in January 2026 and be capped at \$6,500, but with this cap rising as indexed to the increase in New Jersey's overall property taxes.[^cite_note-Staysc-208][^cite_note-209]

### Federal taxation disparity

New Jersey consistently ranks as having one of the highest proportional levels of disparity of any state in the U.S., based upon what it receives from the [federal government](195149.md) relative to what it pays. In 2015, [WalletHub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WalletHub) ranked New Jersey the state that was least dependent on federal government aid overall and the state with the fourth-lowest return on taxpayer investment from the federal government, at 48 cents per dollar.[^cite_note-210] In 2023, New Jersey sent approximately \$183 billion to [Washington, D.C.](108956.md) in taxes but only received approximately \$113 billion in federal investment resulting in each state resident sending the federal government approximately \$7,500 more than they get back. In 2025, WalletHub again ranked New Jersey as the least dependent on federal government aid overall with now the second-lowest return on taxpayer spending from the federal government, at 48 cents per dollar.[^cite_note-211]

New Jersey has one of the highest tax burdens of any state the nation.[^cite_note-taxfoundation-212] Factors for this include the large federal tax liability, which is not adjusted for New Jersey's higher cost of living and [Medicaid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicaid) funding formulas.

### Industries

Further information: [Biotech and pharmaceutical companies in New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biotech_and_pharmaceutical_companies_in_New_Jersey)



[Cranberry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cranberry) harvest

New Jersey's economy is multifaceted, featuring high levels of both [productivity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Productivity) and [retail consumption](183515.md); the Garden State's economy comprises the [pharmaceutical industry](560876.md), [biotechnology](4502.md), [information technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_technology), the [financial industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_industry), [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism), [filmmaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filmmaking), [telecommunications](33094374.md), [gambling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gambling), food processing, electrical equipment manufacturing, printing, and publishing. New Jersey's agricultural outputs are nursery stock, horses, vegetables, fruits and nuts, seafood, and dairy products.[^cite_note-213] New Jersey ranks second among states in [blueberry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blueberry) production, third in [cranberries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cranberry) and [spinach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spinach), and fourth in [bell peppers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_peppers), [peaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peach), and [head lettuce](57079.md).[^cite_note-214] The state harvests the fourth-largest number of acres planted with [asparagus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asparagus).[^cite_note-215] [South Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Jersey) has become an [East Coast epicenter](89126.md) for [logistics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logistics) and [warehouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warehouse) construction.[^cite_note-sjepi-216] In 2025, 99.7% of businesses in New Jersey were [small businesses](276495.md), employing 48.8% of the state's workforce.[^cite_note-217]

#### Scientific economy

New Jersey has a strong [scientific economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientist) and is home to major pharmaceutical and telecommunications firms, drawing on the state's large and well-educated labor pool, including one of the highest concentrations of [engineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engineer) and other scientists in the world. There is also a robust service economy in retail sales, education, and real estate, serving residents who work in New York City or Philadelphia. [Thomas Edison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Edison) invented the first [electric light bulb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_light_bulb) at his home in [Menlo Park, Edison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menlo_Park,_New_Jersey) in 1879. New Jersey is also a key participant in the [renewable wind industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_industry). New Jersey has more scientists and engineers per square mile than anywhere in the world,[^cite_note-218] and is a global leader in [pharmaceuticals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pharmaceuticals), [biotechnology](4502.md), [life sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_sciences), and [technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technology).[^cite_note-219][^cite_note-220]

#### Corporate and retail

New Jersey hosts numerous business headquarters, including twenty-four [Fortune 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) companies.[^cite_note-221] [Paramus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramus,_New_Jersey) in [Bergen County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bergen_County,_New_Jersey) has become the top retail [ZIP code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ZIP_code) (07652) in the United States, with the municipality generating over US\$6 billion in annual retail sales.[^cite_note-para-222] Several New Jersey counties, including [Somerset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somerset_County,_New_Jersey) (7), [Morris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morris_County,_New_Jersey) (10), [Hunterdon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunterdon_County,_New_Jersey) (13), Bergen (21), and [Monmouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monmouth_County,_New_Jersey) (42), have been ranked among the [highest-income counties in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highest-income_counties_in_the_United_States).

#### Shipping, manufacturing, and logistics



The [Port Newark-Elizabeth Marine Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Newark-Elizabeth_Marine_Terminal) was the world's first [container seaport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Container_port) and remains one of the world's largest and busiest.

Shipping is a key industry in New Jersey because of the state's strategic geographic location, the [Port of New York and New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_New_York_and_New_Jersey) being the busiest [seaport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seaport) on the [U.S. East Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._East_Coast). The [Port Newark-Elizabeth Marine Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Newark-Elizabeth_Marine_Terminal) was the world's first [container port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Container_port) and today is one of the world's largest and busiest. New Jersey's location at the center of the [Eastern North American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_North_America) population belt has made the state a prime hub for the logistics, warehousing, and [supply chain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supply_chain) management industries. The [manufacturing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacturing) economy in New Jersey had declined for several decades in the post-[Industrial Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_Revolution) era but has since resumed growth.

#### Tourism



[Sunset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunset) on the beach at [Atlantic City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_City,_New_Jersey), an [oceanfront resort](293990.md) and the nexus of New Jersey's [gambling industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casino)

New Jersey's location at the center of the [Northeast megalopolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_megalopolis) and its extensive transportation system have put over one-third of all United States residents and many Canadian residents within overnight distance by land. This accessibility to consumer revenue has enabled [seaside resorts](293990.md) such as [Atlantic City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_City,_New_Jersey) and the remainder of the [Jersey Shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_Shore), as well as the state's other natural and cultural attractions, to contribute significantly to the record 111 million tourist visits to New Jersey in 2018, providing US\$44.7 billion in tourism revenue, directly supporting 333,860 jobs, sustaining more than 531,000 jobs overall including peripheral impacts, and generating US\$5 billion in state and local tax revenue.[^cite_note-223]

#### Gambling

Main article: [Gambling in New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gambling_in_New_Jersey)

In 1976, a [referendum](51582.md) by Jersey voters approved [casino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casino) gambling in Atlantic City, where the first legalized casino opened in 1978.[^cite_note-224] At that time, [Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Vegas_Valley) was the only other casino resort in the country.[^cite_note-225] Today, several casinos lie along the [Atlantic City Boardwalk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_City_Boardwalk), the oldest and longest boardwalk in the world, at 5+1⁄2 miles (8.9 km) in length.[^cite_note-Oyster2015-226] Atlantic City experienced a dramatic contraction in its stature as a gambling destination after 2010, including the closure of multiple casinos since 2014, spurred by competition from the advent of legalized gambling in other northeastern U.S. states.[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228]

On February 26, 2013, Governor Chris Christie signed [online gambling](2213921.md) into law.[^cite_note-229] [Sports betting](1328050.md) has become a growing source of gambling revenue in New Jersey, with sportsbooks bringing in almost \$12 billion in bets, making over \$1 billion in revenue in 2023. Since being legalized across the nation by the [U.S. Supreme Court](31737.md) on May 14, 2018, New Jersey led all states in sports betting handle until New York passed them.[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231] In September 2022, the lifetime revenue from [online casinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Online_casinos) operating in New Jersey for the nine years since the industry's launch had surpassed \$5 billion.[^cite_note-232]

#### Media



New Jersey's telephone [area codes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telephone_numbering_plan)

##### Television and film production

Main article: [Television and film of New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_and_film_of_New_Jersey)

New Jersey is a growing center for [filmmaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filmmaking) and [television production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_production),[^cite_note-NHub1-233] with [media companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_media), enticed by its proximity to [Manhattan](45470.md), in conjunction with [tax incentives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_incentive), collectively spending billions of dollars to develop large new [studio facilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_studio) and [sound stage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sound_stage) complexes.[^cite_note-NHub2-234] Motion picture technology was developed by [Thomas Edison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Edison), with much of his early work done at his [West Orange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Orange,_New_Jersey) laboratory. [Edison's Black Maria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edison's_Black_Maria) was the first motion picture studio. [America's first motion picture industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/America's_first_motion_picture_industry) started in 1907 in [Fort Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Lee,_New_Jersey) and the first studio was constructed there in 1909.[^cite_note-235] [DuMont Laboratories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DuMont_Laboratories) in [Passaic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passaic,_New_Jersey) developed early sets and made the first broadcast to the private home.

A number of television shows and films have been filmed in New Jersey. Since 1978, the state has maintained a Motion Picture and Television Commission to encourage filming in-state.[^cite_note-236] New Jersey has long offered tax credits to television producers. Governor Chris Christie suspended the credits in 2010, but the [New Jersey State Legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_State_Legislature) in 2011 approved the restoration and expansion of the tax credit program. Under bills passed by both the state Senate and Assembly, the program offers 20 percent tax credits (22% in [urban enterprise zones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_enterprise_zone)) to television and film productions that shoot in the state and meet set standards for hiring and local spending.[^cite_note-crewsback-237] When Governor Phil Murphy took office, he instated the New Jersey Film & Digital Media Tax Credit Program in 2018 and expanded it in 2020. The benefits include a 30% tax credit on film projects and a 40% subsidy for studio developments.[^cite_note-238]

##### Radio stations

Main article: [List of radio stations in New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_radio_stations_in_New_Jersey)

##### Television stations

New Jersey has several [PBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PBS) member stations: [WNET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNET) (13) in [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey), [WNJN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNJN_\(TV\)) (50) in [Montclair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montclair,_New_Jersey), WNJB (58) in [New Brunswick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Brunswick,_NJ), WNJS (23) in [Camden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camden,_New_Jersey) and WNJT (52) in [Trenton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton,_NJ).

There are no standard commercial [network affiliates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Network_affiliate) in the state. [WMGM-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WMGM-TV) ([Wildwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildwood,_New_Jersey)) lost its affiliation with [NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC) in 2014. Viewers in northern New Jersey receive [New York City market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_New_York_City#Television) stations over cable or over the air; southern New Jersey viewers receive [Philadelphia market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Philadelphia#Television_stations) stations over cable or over the air.

WMGM now affiliates with the [True Crime Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True_Crime_Network). [WJLP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WJLP) ([Middletown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middletown,_New_Jersey)) affiliates with the retro network [MeTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MeTV). There are [Telemundo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telemundo) affiliates in Fort Lee, Linden and Mount Laurel, and [Univision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Univision) affiliates in Paterson and Vineland.

#### Finance as Wall Street West



The developing waterfront skyline of [Downtown Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Jersey_City), dubbed [Wall Street West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street_West)

[^cite_note-jwall-242]

[Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City)'s [Hudson River waterfront](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Jersey_City), from [Exchange Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exchange_Place_\(Jersey_City\)) to [Newport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newport,_Jersey_City), is known as [Wall Street West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street_West)[^cite_note-jwall-242] and has over 13 million square feet of [Class A office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Class_A_office) space. One third of the [private sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_sector) jobs in the city are in the [financial services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finance) sector: more than 60% are in the [securities industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_\(finance\)), 20% are in banking and 8% in [insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurance).[^cite_note-243] Jersey City is home to the headquarters of [Verisk Analytics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verisk_Analytics) and [Lord Abbett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lord_Abbett),[^cite_note-StarLedger-244] a privately held money management firm.[^cite_note-245] Companies such as [Computershare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computershare), [ADP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automatic_Data_Processing), [IPC Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPC_Systems), and [Fidelity Investments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidelity_Investments) also conduct operations in the city.[^cite_note-246] In 2014, *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes)* magazine moved its headquarters to the district, having been awarded a \$27 million tax grant in exchange for bringing 350 jobs to the city over a ten-year period.[^cite_note-247] By the early 2020s, the construction of residential [skyscrapers](29485.md) Downtown made median rental rates in Jersey City amongst the highest of any city in the United States.[^cite_note-248]

#### Natural resources and energy

See also: [List of power stations in New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_power_stations_in_New_Jersey)

Limited mining activity of [zinc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinc), iron, and [manganese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manganese) still takes place in the area in and around the [Franklin Furnace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_Furnace) in [Sussex County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sussex_County,_New_Jersey).

Although New Jersey is home to many energy-intensive industries, its energy consumption is only 2.7% of the U.S. total, and its [carbon dioxide emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_dioxide_emissions) are 0.8% of the U.S. total. New Jersey's electricity comes primarily from natural gas and nuclear power.[^cite_note-249] New Jersey is seventh in the nation in [solar power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_power_in_New_Jersey) installations,[^cite_note-250] enabled by one of the country's most favorable [net metering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_metering) policies and [renewable portfolio standard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_portfolio_standards_in_the_United_States). The state has more than 140,000 solar installations.[^cite_note-251]

## Environment

Due to past industrial activity, New Jersey has more [Superfund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superfund) toxic waste sites than any other state in the union despite its small geographic size. By 2024, only 35 of New Jersey's Superfund sites (out of about 150 that have been on the EPA's list since the Superfund law was passed in 1980) have been cleaned up.[^cite_note-NPL2024-252]

In late 2023, a concern became public about [PFAs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Per-_and_polyfluoroalkyl_substances) (so-called "forever chemicals") existing in the state's water supplies.[^cite_note-PFAS-253]

## Transportation

Main article: [Transportation in New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_New_Jersey)

New Jersey's population density and location at the geographic center of the [Northeast Megalopolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Megalopolis) have rendered it a vital transportation for hub for both passengers and industry.

### Roadways

See also: [State highways in New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_highways_in_New_Jersey)



Map of New Jersey's major transportation networks and cities



The [George Washington Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington_Bridge), connecting [Fort Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Lee,_New_Jersey) (foreground) in [Bergen County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bergen_County,_New_Jersey) across the [Hudson River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hudson_River) to [New York City](645042.md) (background), is the world's busiest motor vehicle bridge.

[^cite_note-panynj.gov-254]

[^cite_note-abcgwb-255]

The [New Jersey Turnpike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Turnpike) is one of the most prominent and heavily trafficked roadways in the United States. This [toll road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toll_road), which overlaps with [Interstate 95](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_95_in_New_Jersey) for much of its length, carries traffic between [Delaware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware) and New York, and up and down the [East Coast](89126.md) in general. Commonly referred to as simply "the Turnpike", it is known for its numerous [rest areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rest_area) named after prominent New Jerseyans.

The [Garden State Parkway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garden_State_Parkway), or simply "the Parkway", carries relatively more in-state traffic than interstate traffic and runs from New Jersey's northern border to its southernmost tip at [Cape May](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_May,_New_Jersey). It is the main route that connects the [New York metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_metropolitan_area) to the [Jersey Shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_Shore). With a total of fifteen travel and six shoulder lanes, the [Driscoll Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Driscoll_Bridge) on the Parkway, spanning the [Raritan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raritan_River) in [Middlesex County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middlesex_County,_New_Jersey), is the widest motor vehicle bridge in the world by number of lanes as well as one of the busiest.[^cite_note-256]

New Jersey is connected to New York City via various key bridges and tunnels. The double-decked [George Washington Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington_Bridge) carries the heaviest load of motor vehicle traffic of any bridge in the world, at 102 million vehicles per year, across fourteen lanes.[^cite_note-panynj.gov-254][^cite_note-abcgwb-255] It connects [Fort Lee, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Lee,_New_Jersey) to the [Washington Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Heights,_Manhattan) neighborhood of [Upper Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Manhattan), and carries [Interstate 95](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_95) and [U.S. Route 1/9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_1/9) across the [Hudson River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hudson_River). The [Lincoln Tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Tunnel) connects to [Midtown Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midtown_Manhattan) carrying [New Jersey Route 495](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Route_495), and the [Holland Tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holland_Tunnel) connects to [Lower Manhattan](455646.md) carrying [Interstate 78](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_78_in_New_Jersey). New Jersey is also connected to [Staten Island](127062.md) by three bridges—from north to south, the [Bayonne Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayonne_Bridge), the [Goethals Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goethals_Bridge), and the [Outerbridge Crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outerbridge_Crossing).

New Jersey has [interstate compacts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_compact) with all three of its neighboring states. The [Port Authority of New York and New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_of_New_York_and_New_Jersey), the [Delaware River Port Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_River_Port_Authority) (with [Pennsylvania](23332.md)), the [Delaware River Joint Toll Bridge Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_River_Joint_Toll_Bridge_Commission) (with Pennsylvania), and the [Delaware River and Bay Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_River_and_Bay_Authority) (with [Delaware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware)) operate most of the major transportation routes in and out of the state. Bridge tolls are collected only from traffic exiting the state, with the exception of the private [Dingman's Ferry Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dingman's_Ferry_Bridge) over the Delaware River, which charges a toll in both directions.

It is unlawful for a customer to serve themselves gasoline in New Jersey. It became the last remaining U.S. state where all [gas stations](61260.md) are required to sell full-service gasoline to customers at all times in 2016, after [Oregon](26811621.md)'s introduction of restricted self-service gasoline availability took effect.[^cite_note-257]

### Airports

[Newark Liberty International Airport](248492.md) (EWR) is one of the [busiest airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_busiest_airports_in_the_United_States) in the United States. Operated by the [Port Authority of New York and New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_of_New_York_and_New_Jersey), it is one of the three main airports serving the [New York metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_metropolitan_area), along with [John F. Kennedy International Airport](97218.md) and [LaGuardia Airport](248547.md), which are both in Queens, New York. [United Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines) is the airport's largest tenant, operating an entire terminal (Terminal C) there, which it uses as one of its primary [hubs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airline_hub). [FedEx Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FedEx_Express) operates a large [cargo terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cargo_terminal) at EWR as well. The adjacent [Newark Airport railroad station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark_Liberty_International_Airport_\(NJT_station\)) provides access to [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) and [NJ Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NJ_Transit) trains along the [Northeast Corridor Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Corridor_Line).

Two smaller commercial airports, [Atlantic City International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_City_International_Airport) and rapidly growing [Trenton-Mercer Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton-Mercer_Airport), also operate in other parts of the state. [Teterboro Airport](475231.md) in [Bergen County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bergen_County,_New_Jersey) and [Millville Municipal Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millville_Municipal_Airport) in [Cumberland County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cumberland_County,_New_Jersey) are [general aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_aviation) airports popular with [private](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_aviation) and [corporate aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporate_aviation) due to their proximity to New York City and the [Jersey Shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_Shore), respectively.

### Rail and bus

Main article: [NJ Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NJ_Transit)

Further information: [New Jersey Transit Bus Operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Transit_Bus_Operations), [New Jersey Transit Rail Operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Transit_Rail_Operations), [Port Authority Trans-Hudson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_Trans-Hudson), [PATCO Speedline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PATCO_Speedline), and [List of New Jersey railroads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_New_Jersey_railroads)



Two [Hudson–Bergen Light Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hudson%E2%80%93Bergen_Light_Rail) trains in [Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City,_New_Jersey)



A [NJ Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NJ_Transit) train on the [Northeast Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Corridor) in [Rahway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahway,_New_Jersey)

[NJ Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NJ_Transit) operates extensive rail and bus service throughout the state. A state-run corporation, it began with the consolidation of several private bus companies in [North Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Jersey) in 1979. In the early 1980s, it acquired [Conrail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conrail)'s commuter train operations that connected suburban towns to New York City. NJ Transit has [12 rail lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NJ_Transit_Rail_Operations) that run through different parts of the state and 165 stations statewide.[^cite_note-258] Most of the lines end at either [New York Penn Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Penn_Station) in [Midtown Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midtown_Manhattan) or [Hoboken Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoboken_Terminal) in [Hoboken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoboken,_New_Jersey), although some lines provide service to both terminal stations. The [Atlantic City Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_City_Line) provides service between [Atlantic City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_City,_New_Jersey) and [Philadelphia](50585.md).[^cite_note-259]

NJ Transit also operates three [light rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_rail) systems in the state. The [Hudson-Bergen Light Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hudson-Bergen_Light_Rail) connects [Bayonne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayonne,_New_Jersey) to [North Bergen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Bergen,_New_Jersey), through [Hoboken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoboken,_New_Jersey) and [Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City,_New_Jersey).[^cite_note-260] The [Newark Light Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark_Light_Rail) is partially underground, and connects downtown [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey) with other parts of the city and its suburbs, [Belleville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belleville,_New_Jersey) and [Bloomfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomfield,_New_Jersey). The [River Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/River_Line_\(New_Jersey_Transit\)) connects [Trenton](57843.md) and [Camden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camden,_New_Jersey).

The [PATH](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_Trans-Hudson) is a [rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) system consisting of four lines operated by the [Port Authority of New York and New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_of_New_York_and_New_Jersey). It links [Hoboken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoboken,_New_Jersey), [Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City,_New_Jersey), [Harrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrison,_New_Jersey), and [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey) with New York City. The [PATCO Speedline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PATCO_Speedline) is a rapid transit system that links [Camden County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camden_County,_New_Jersey) to Philadelphia. Both the PATCO and the PATH are two of only five rapid transit systems in the United States to operate 24 hours a day.

[Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) operates numerous long-distance passenger trains in New Jersey, both to and from neighboring states and around the country. In addition to the Newark Airport connection, other major Amtrak railway stations include [Trenton Transit Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton_\(Amtrak_station\)), [Metropark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropark_\(NJT_station\)), and the historic [Newark Penn Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark_Penn_Station).

The [Southeastern Pennsylvania Transportation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEPTA), or SEPTA, has two commuter rail lines that operate into New Jersey. The [Trenton Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton_Line) terminates at the [Trenton Transit Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton_Transit_Center), and the [West Trenton Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Trenton_Line_\(SEPTA\)) terminates at the [West Trenton Rail Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Trenton_Rail_Station) in [Ewing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ewing,_New_Jersey).

[AirTrain Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AirTrain_Newark) is a [monorail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monorail) connecting the Amtrak/NJ Transit station on the Northeast Corridor to the airport's terminals and parking lots.

Some private bus carriers still remain in New Jersey. Most of these carriers operate with state funding to offset losses and state owned buses are provided to these carriers, of which [Coach USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coach_USA) companies make up the bulk. Other carriers include private charter and tour bus operators that take gamblers from other parts of New Jersey, New York City, Philadelphia, and [Delaware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware) to the casino resorts of Atlantic City.

### Ferries



[Cape May–Lewes Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_May%E2%80%93Lewes_Ferry) connects New Jersey and [Delaware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware) across [Delaware Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_Bay).

[New York Waterway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NY_Waterway) has ferry terminals at [Belford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belford,_New_Jersey), [Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City), Hoboken, [Weehawken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weehawken), and [Edgewater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgewater,_New_Jersey), with service to different parts of Manhattan. [Liberty Water Taxi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_Water_Taxi) in [Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City) has ferries from [Paulus Hook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paulus_Hook) and [Liberty State Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_State_Park) to [Battery Park City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battery_Park_City) in Manhattan. [Statue Cruises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statue_Cruises) offers service from Liberty State Park to the [Statue of Liberty National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statue_of_Liberty_National_Monument), including [Ellis Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellis_Island). [SeaStreak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SeaStreak) offers services from the [Raritan Bayshore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raritan_Bayshore) to Manhattan, [Martha's Vineyard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martha's_Vineyard), and [Nantucket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nantucket).

The [Delaware River and Bay Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_River_and_Bay_Authority) operates the [Cape May–Lewes Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cape_May%E2%80%93Lewes_Ferry) on [Delaware Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_Bay), carrying both passengers and vehicles between New Jersey and Delaware as part of [US 9](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_9). The agency also operates the [Forts Ferry Crossing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forts_Ferry_Crossing) for passengers across the [Delaware River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_River). The [Delaware River Port Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delaware_River_Port_Authority) operates the [RiverLink Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RiverLink_Ferry) between the [Camden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camden,_New_Jersey) waterfront and [Penn's Landing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penn's_Landing) in Philadelphia.

## Culture



[New Brunswick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Brunswick,_New_Jersey), nicknamed the *Hub city* and the *Healthcare City*, is a focus city for academia, [healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare), and culture in New Jersey, and has many [high-rise](118767.md) buildings.



A 1950s-style [diner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diner) in [Orange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orange,_New_Jersey)

### General

New Jersey has continued to play a prominent role as a U.S. cultural nexus. The state has its own [cuisines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_New_Jersey), religious communities, [museums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_New_Jersey), and [halls of fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Halls_of_fame_in_New_Jersey).

New Jersey is the birthplace of many modern inventions, including [FM radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FM_radio), the motion picture camera, the [lithium battery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithium_battery), the [light bulb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_bulb), [transistors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transistor), and the electric train. Other New Jersey creations include: the drive-in movie, the cultivated [blueberry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blueberry), [cranberry sauce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cranberry_sauce), the [boardwalk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boardwalk), the [zipper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zipper), the [phonograph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phonograph), [saltwater taffy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saltwater_taffy), the [dirigible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirigible), the [seedless watermelon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watermelon),[^cite_note-261] the [first use](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Bushnell_\(inventor\)) of a [submarine](28825.md) in warfare, and the [ice cream cone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ice_cream_cone).[^cite_note-262]

[Diners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diner) are iconic to New Jersey. The state is home to many diner manufacturers and has over 600 diners, more than any other place in the world.[^cite_note-263]

New Jersey is the only state to have never had a [state song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._state_songs);[^cite_note-264] as of 2021, it is one of only two states (the other being [Maryland](18858.md)[^cite_note-265]) that are currently without a state song. "I'm From New Jersey" is incorrectly listed on many websites as being the New Jersey state song, but it was not even a contender when the New Jersey Arts Council submitted state song suggestions to the [New Jersey Legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Legislature) in 1996.[^cite_note-266]

New Jersey is frequently the target of jokes in American culture,[^cite_note-267] especially from New York City-based television shows, such as *[Saturday Night Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saturday_Night_Live)*.[^cite_note-Rockland-268] Academic [Michael Aaron Rockland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Aaron_Rockland) attributes this to New Yorkers' view that New Jersey is the beginning of [Middle America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_America_\(United_States\)). The [New Jersey Turnpike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Turnpike), which runs between two major East Coast cities, New York City and Philadelphia, is also cited as a reason, as people who traverse through the state may only see its industrial zones.[^cite_note-sjt-269] Reality television shows like *[Jersey Shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_Shore_\(TV_series\))* and *[The Real Housewives of New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Real_Housewives_of_New_Jersey)* have reinforced stereotypical views of New Jersey culture,[^cite_note-270] but Rockland cited *[The Sopranos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sopranos)* and the music of [Bruce Springsteen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Springsteen) as exporting a more positive image.[^cite_note-sjt-269]

The "New" in "New Jersey" is often omitted in casual conversation.[^cite_note-271]

### Cuisine

Main article: [Cuisine of New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_New_Jersey)

New Jersey is known for several foods developed within the region, including [Taylor Ham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Pork_Roll) (also known as [pork roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pork_roll)), [sloppy joe sandwiches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sloppy_joe_\(New_Jersey\)), [tomato pies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton_tomato_pie), [salt water taffy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taffy_\(candy\)), and [Texas wieners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chili_dog). Just as [New York City's cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_New_York_City) has an influence on [North Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Jersey), [Philadelphia's cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_Philadelphia) influences [South Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Jersey).

New Jersey's third-largest industry is food and agriculture just behind pharmaceuticals and tourism. New Jersey is one of the top 10 producers of [blueberries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blueberry), [cranberries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cranberry), [peaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peach), [tomatoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomato), [bell peppers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bell_pepper), [eggplant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eggplant), [cucumbers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cucumber), [apples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple), [spinach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spinach), [squash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Squash_\(plant\)), and [asparagus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asparagus) in the United States. Many restaurants in the state offer locally grown ingredients because of this.[^cite_note-272]

[Campbell's Soup Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campbell's_Soup_Company) has been headquartered in [Camden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camden,_New_Jersey) since 1869.[^cite_note-camp-273] [Goya Foods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goya_Foods), the largest [Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic)-owned food company in the United States, operates a corporate headquarters in [Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City,_New_Jersey).[^cite_note-274] [Mars Wrigley Confectionery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wrigley_Company)'s US headquarters has been based in [Hackettstown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hackettstown,_New_Jersey) and [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey) since 2007.[^cite_note-275]

Several states with substantial Italian American populations take credit for the development of [submarine sandwiches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Submarine_sandwich), including New Jersey.[^cite_note-276]

### Music

Main article: [Music of New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_New_Jersey)

New Jersey has long been an important origin for both [rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_and_roll) and [rap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip-hop) music. Prominent musicians from or with significant connections to New Jersey include:

- Singer [Frank Sinatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Sinatra) was born in [Hoboken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoboken,_New_Jersey). He sang with a neighborhood vocal group, the Hoboken Four, and appeared in neighborhood theater amateur shows before he became an [Academy Award](324.md)-winning actor.

- Singer [Whitney Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitney_Houston) was born in [Newark, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey). Houston began singing at [New Hope Baptist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Hope_Baptist_Church_\(Newark\)) in Newark as a child and became a background vocalist while in high school. She then became the [best-selling New Jersey musician ever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_best-selling_music_artists), having sold over 220 million records worldwide.

- [Bruce Springsteen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Springsteen), who has sung of New Jersey life on most of his albums, is from [Freehold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freehold_Borough,_New_Jersey). Some of his songs that represent New Jersey life are "[Born to Run](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Born_to_Run_\(song\))", "Spirit in the Night", "[Rosalita (Come Out Tonight)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosalita_\(Come_Out_Tonight\))", "[Thunder Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thunder_Road_\(song\))", "[Atlantic City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_City_\(song\))", and "[Jungleland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jungleland)".

- [Irvington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irvington,_New_Jersey)'s [Queen Latifah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Latifah) was one of the first female rappers to succeed in music, film, and television.

  [^cite_note-277]

- [Southside Johnny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southside_Johnny), eponymous leader of Southside Johnny and the Asbury Jukes was raised in [Ocean Grove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocean_Grove,_NJ). He is considered the "Grandfather of the New Jersey Sound"

  [^cite_note-278]

  &#x20;and is cited by Jersey-born [Jon Bon Jovi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Bon_Jovi) as his reason for singing.

  [^cite_note-279]

- [Jon Bon Jovi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Bon_Jovi), from [Sayreville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sayreville,_New_Jersey), reached fame in the 1980s with hard rock outfit [Bon Jovi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bon_Jovi). The band has also written many songs about life in New Jersey, including "Livin' On A Prayer",

  [^cite_note-280]

  &#x20;and named [one of their albums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_\(album\)) after the state.

- Grammy-winning jazz vocalist [Sarah Vaughan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarah_Vaughan) was born and raised in [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey). After singing in her church's choir as a child, she was sneaking into Newark nightclubs to sing and play piano by the time she was a teenager.

  [^cite_note-281]

- In 1964, [the Isley Brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Isley_Brothers) founded the record label [T-Neck Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-Neck_Records), named after [Teaneck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teaneck,_New_Jersey), their home at the time.

  [^cite_note-282]

- The Broadway musical *[Jersey Boys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_Boys)* is based on the lives of the members of the [Four Seasons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Four_Seasons_\(group\)), three of whose members were born in New Jersey ([Tommy DeVito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_DeVito_\(musician\)), [Frankie Valli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankie_Valli), and [Nick Massi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Massi)) while a fourth, [Bob Gaudio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Gaudio), was born in [the Bronx](3338.md) but raised in [Bergenfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bergenfield,_New_Jersey).

  [^cite_note-283]

## Sports

Main article: [Sports in New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_New_Jersey)



[MetLife Stadium](5476983.md) in [East Rutherford](136878.md), one of only two [NFL](21211.md) stadiums shared by two teams, is home to the [New York Giants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Giants) and [New York Jets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Jets).

New Jersey currently has six teams from [major professional sports leagues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_professional_sports_leagues_in_the_United_States_and_Canada) playing in the state with only one team identifying themselves as solely being from New Jersey. The remaining five teams identify themselves as being from the [New York metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_metropolitan_area) with the [National Women's Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Women's_Soccer_League) team having a team name that represents both New Jersey and New York. The National Hockey League and [National Basketball Association](22093.md) teams representing [Philadelphia](50585.md) have their training facilities in [South Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Jersey).

### Professional sports



[Prudential Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prudential_Center) in [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey), home of the [NHL](21809.md)'s [New Jersey Devils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Devils)

The [National Hockey League](21809.md)'s [New Jersey Devils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Devils), based in [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey) at the [Prudential Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prudential_Center), is the only major league [sports franchise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_franchise) to bear the state's name. Founded in 1974 in [Kansas City, Missouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City,_Missouri), as the [Kansas City Scouts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kansas_City_Scouts), the team played in [Denver, Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver,_Colorado), as the [Colorado Rockies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Rockies_\(NHL\)) from 1976 until the spring of 1982 when naval architect, businessman, and [Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City) native [John J. McMullen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_J._McMullen) purchased, renamed, and moved the franchise to [Brendan Byrne Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brendan_Byrne_Arena) in [East Rutherford's](136878.md) [Meadowlands Sports Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meadowlands_Sports_Complex). While the team was poor to mediocre in Kansas City, Denver, and its first years in New Jersey, qualifying for the playoffs once in the 13 seasons from 1974 to 1987, the Devils ultimately established themselves in late 1980s and early 1990s during the tenure of Hall of Fame president and general manager [Lou Lamoriello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lou_Lamoriello). As of 2023, the Devils have appeared in 23 postseasons in 40 seasons in New Jersey, reaching five [Stanley Cup](66968.md) Finals (most recently in [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Stanley_Cup_playoffs)) and winning it in [1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_Stanley_Cup_playoffs), [2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Stanley_Cup_playoffs), and [2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003_Stanley_Cup_playoffs). The organization is the youngest of the nine "Big Four" major league teams based in New York metropolitan area, ultimately establishing its core following throughout the northern and central portions of the state and carving a place in a media market once dominated by the [New York Rangers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Rangers) and [Islanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Islanders) which has the distinction of being the only metropolitan area in the country with three major league professional sports teams participating in the same sport.

In 2018, the [Philadelphia Flyers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_Flyers) renovated and expanded their training facility, the Virtua Center Flyers Skate Zone, in [Voorhees Township](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voorhees_Township,_New_Jersey) in the southern portion of the state.[^cite_note-284]

In 2024, the [New York Sirens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Sirens) of the [Professional Women's Hockey League](74699992.md) (PWHL) made the Prudential Center their home rink and moved their practice facility to the [Richard J. Codey Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_J._Codey_Arena) in [West Orange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Orange,_New_Jersey), the former Devil's practice facility from 1986 to 2007.[^cite_note-285]

The [New York metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_metropolitan_area)'s two [National Football League](21211.md) teams, the [New York Giants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Giants) and the [New York Jets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Jets), play at [MetLife Stadium](5476983.md) in [East Rutherford's](136878.md) [Meadowlands Sports Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meadowlands_Sports_Complex).[^cite_note-stad-286] Built for about \$1.6 billion,[^cite_note-Belson-287] the venue is one of the most expensive stadiums ever built.[^cite_note-Esteban-288] On February 2, 2014, MetLife Stadium hosted [Super Bowl XLVIII](4057023.md).

The [New York Red Bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Red_Bulls) of [Major League Soccer](71802.md) play in [Sports Illustrated Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_Illustrated_Stadium), a soccer-specific stadium in [Harrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrison,_New_Jersey) across the [Passaic River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passaic_River) from downtown [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey). On July 27, 2011, Red Bull Arena hosted the [2011 MLS All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_MLS_All-Star_Game).[^cite_note-289] [Gotham FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gotham_FC) of the [National Women's Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Women's_Soccer_League) (NWSL), founded in 2008 as Sky Blue FC, moved its home field from [Yurcak Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yurcak_Field) at [Rutgers University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutgers_University) to Sports Illustrated Stadium in 2020.[^cite_note-290]

New Jersey hosted matches during the [1994 FIFA World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup) at [Giants Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giants_Stadium). Over three decades later, New Jersey hosted matches during the [2026 FIFA World Cup](36581929.md), at [MetLife Stadium](5476983.md), including the [tournament final](75845879.md).[^cite_note-291]

From 1977 to 2012, New Jersey had a [National Basketball Association](22093.md) team, the [New Jersey Nets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Nets).[^cite_note-292] [WNBA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WNBA)'s [New York Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Liberty) played in New Jersey from 2011 to 2013 while their primary home arena, [Madison Square Garden](75129.md) was undergoing renovations.[^cite_note-293] In 2016, the [Philadelphia 76ers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_76ers) of the NBA opened their new headquarters and training facility, the Philadelphia 76ers Training Complex, in [Camden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camden,_New_Jersey).[^cite_note-294]

The Meadowlands Sports Complex is home to the [Meadowlands Racetrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meadowlands_Racetrack), the major [harness racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harness_racing) track in the state. [Monmouth Park Racetrack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monmouth_Park_Racetrack) in Oceanport is a popular spot for [thoroughbred racing](67745.md) in New Jersey and the northeast. It hosted the [Breeders' Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breeders'_Cup) in 2007, and its turf course was renovated in preparation.

### College sports

See also: [List of college athletic programs in New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_college_athletic_programs_in_New_Jersey)

#### Major schools

New Jerseyans' collegiate allegiances are predominantly split among the three major [NCAA Division I](669931.md) programs in the state: the [Rutgers University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutgers_University%E2%80%93New_Brunswick) (New Jersey's flagship state university) [Scarlet Knights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutgers_Scarlet_Knights), members of the [Big Ten Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Ten_Conference); the [Seton Hall University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seton_Hall_University) (the state's largest [Catholic](410868.md) university) [Pirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seton_Hall_Pirates), members of the [Big East Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_East_Conference); and the [Princeton University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princeton_University) (the state's [Ivy League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivy_League) university) [Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princeton_Tigers).

The intense rivalry between Rutgers and Princeton athletics began with the [first intercollegiate football game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1869_college_football_season) in 1869. The schools have not met on the football field since 1980, but they continue to play each other annually in all other sports offered by the two universities.

Rutgers, which fields 24 teams in various sports, is nationally known for its football program, with a 6–4 all-time [bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bowl_game) record; and its women's basketball programs, which appeared in a [National Final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_NCAA_Division_I_women's_basketball_tournament) in 2007. In 2008 and 2009, Rutgers expanded their football home, [Rutgers Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SHI_Stadium), now called SHI Stadium, on the [Busch Campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Busch_Campus_of_Rutgers_University). The basketball teams play at the [Rutgers Athletic Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutgers_Athletic_Center) on [Livingston Campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livingston_Campus_\(Rutgers_University\)). Both venues and campuses are in [Piscataway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piscataway_Township,_New_Jersey), across the [Raritan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raritan_River) from [New Brunswick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Brunswick,_New_Jersey). The university also fields men's basketball and baseball programs. Rutgers' fans live mostly in the western parts of the state and [Middlesex County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middlesex_County,_New_Jersey); its alumni base is the largest in the state.

Rutgers' satellite campuses in Camden and Newark each field their own athletic programs—the [Rutgers–Camden Scarlet Raptors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutgers%E2%80%93Camden_Scarlet_Raptors) and the [Rutgers–Newark Scarlet Raiders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rutgers%E2%80%93Newark_Scarlet_Raiders)—which both compete in NCAA [Division III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Division_III_\(NCAA\)).

Seton Hall fields no football team, but its men's basketball team is one of the [Big East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_East_Conference)'s storied programs. No New Jersey team has won more games in the [NCAA Division I men's basketball tournament](507399.md), and it is the state's only men's basketball program to reach a modern [National Final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_NCAA_Division_I_men's_basketball_tournament). The Pirates play their home games at [Prudential Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prudential_Center) in downtown [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey), about 4 miles (6 km) from the university's [South Orange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Orange,_New_Jersey) campus. Their fans hail largely from the predominantly Roman Catholic areas of the northern part of the state and the [Jersey Shore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_Shore). The annual inter-conference rivalry game between Seton Hall and Rutgers, whose venue alternates between Newark and Piscataway, the Garden State Hardwood Classic, is planned through 2026.[^cite_note-295]

#### Other schools

The state's other Division I schools include the [Monmouth University Hawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monmouth_Hawks) (West Long Branch), the [New Jersey Institute of Technology (NJIT) Highlanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NJIT_Mens_Soccer) (Newark), the [Rider University Broncs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rider_University#Athletics) (Lawrenceville), and the [Saint Peter's University Peacocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Peter's_College,_New_Jersey#Athletics) (Jersey City).

[Fairleigh Dickinson University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairleigh_Dickinson_Knights) competes in both Division I and Division III. It has two campuses, each with its own sports teams. The teams at the Metropolitan Campus are known as the FDU Knights, and compete in the [Northeast Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Conference) and NCAA Division I. The college at Florham (FDU-Florham) teams are known as the FDU-Florham Devils and compete in the [Middle Atlantic Conferences' Freedom Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Atlantic_Conferences#Freedom_Conference) and NCAA Division III.

Among the various Division III schools in the state, the [Stevens Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stevens_Tech_Ducks_football) Ducks have fielded the longest continuously running collegiate men's lacrosse program in the country. 2009 marked the 125th season.

### High school

New Jersey high schools are divided into divisions under the [New Jersey State Interscholastic Athletic Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_State_Interscholastic_Athletic_Association) (NJSIAA).[^cite_note-296]'[^cite_note-297]
